L3000: Figure 1 displays four plots assessing the goodness of fit for the Final Population PK Model. The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid:
Top Left: Observations vs. Population predictions. The x-axis is 'Population predictions' (0 to 8000) and the y-axis is 'Observations' (0 to 10000). The data points (blue lines) generally follow the red line of identity, indicating a good fit.
Top Right: Observations vs. Individual predictions. The x-axis is 'Individual predictions' (0 to 8000) and the y-axis is 'Observations' (0 to 10000). The data points (blue lines) generally follow the red line of identity, indicating a good fit.
Bottom Left: [WRES] vs. Individual predictions. The x-axis is 'Individual predictions' (0 to 8000) and the y-axis is '[WRES]' (0 to 80). The WRES values are tightly clustered around the zero line, suggesting no systematic bias.
Bottom Right: Conditional weighted residuals vs. Time. The x-axis is 'Time' (0 to 5000) and the y-axis is 'Conditional weighted residuals' (0 to 10). The residuals are randomly scattered around the zero line, indicating no time-dependent trends.
L3001: Figure 2 displays two box plots comparing the distribution of individual subject estimates of the random effect parameters (ETA) based on sex.
Panel A: SEX vs ETA1 (ETA on CL)
This plot shows the distribution of ETA_{CL} (ETA on CL) for females and males. The y-axis ranges from -1.0 to 1.0. The median ETA_{CL} is slightly negative for females (around -0.1) and slightly positive for males (around 0.1). The interquartile range (IQR) is similar for both groups, spanning approximately -0.4 to 0.4. Outliers are visible, particularly for males, with one outlier above 1.0.
Panel B: SEX vs ETA2 (ETA on V)
This plot shows the distribution of ETA_V (ETA on V) for females and males. The y-axis ranges from -1.0 to 1.0. The median ETA_V is slightly negative for females (around -0.1) and slightly positive for males (around 0.1). The IQR is similar for both groups, spanning approximately -0.4 to 0.4. Outliers are visible, particularly for females, with one outlier above 1.0.
L3002: Basic goodness-of-fit plots (Run 28.5)
The figure displays three rows of plots, each containing two plots, illustrating the goodness of fit for the Final Allometric Model.
The top row shows scatter plots of Observations versus Population predictions and Individual predictions. The middle row shows scatter plots of Conditional weighted residuals versus Population predictions and Time. The bottom row shows scatter plots of Conditional weighted residuals versus Time and Time after dose.
In all plots, the data points (blue lines and circles) are scattered around the diagonal line (red line) or the zero line (horizontal red line), indicating a good fit.

L3013: Figure 14 displays two scatter plots comparing ETA1 (Y-axis) versus CRCL (X-axis, representing renal function). L3014: Figure 15 displays two box plots comparing \text{ETA}_{\text{CL}} (Y-axis) against Patient Status (X-axis: Healthy and Patient) before (left panel) and after (right panel) removing Patient Status as a covariate in the model. The legend indicates two groups: FBRYn 0 (red box) and FBRYn 1 (blue box).
Left Panel (Before removal): The distribution of \text{ETA}_{\text{CL}} is similar for both Healthy and Patient groups, centered around 0.0. The median and interquartile range (IQR) are nearly identical.
Right Panel (After removal): The distribution of \text{ETA}_{\text{CL}} remains similar for both Healthy and Patient groups, centered around 0.0. The median and IQR are nearly identical, suggesting that removing Patient Status as a covariate does not significantly alter the distribution of \text{ETA}_{\text{CL}}.
L3015: Figure 16 displays two scatter plots comparing \text{ETA}_{\text{CL}} (Y-axis) against body weight (WT) (X-axis) before (left panel) and after (right panel) removing body weight as a covariate in the model. The plots show individual data points (black dots) and a fitted curve (blue line).
Left Panel (Before removal): The scatter plot shows a clear, non-linear relationship between \text{ETA}_{\text{CL}} and WT. The fitted curve shows a peak around WT 80-90 and a trough around WT 100-110.
Right Panel (After removal): The scatter plot shows a much weaker relationship between \text{ETA}_{\text{CL}} and WT. The fitted curve is flatter, indicating that the effect of body weight on \text{ETA}_{\text{CL}} is minimal or non-existent after removing body weight as a covariate.

The chart illustrates that males generally show a positive change (response) in Lyso-Gb3, particularly among amenable patients (blue bars), while females show a negative change (decrease) in Lyso-Gb3.

L3018: Figure 1 is a line graph showing the Arithmetic Mean (\pmSD) Plasma Concentrations-time profiles for Migalastat across four renal function groups: Normal (N=8), Mild (N=8), Moderate (N=8), and Severe (N=8). The Y-axis represents Migalastat plasma concentration (ng/mL) on a linear scale, ranging from -200 to 3200. The X-axis represents Time postdose (hr), ranging from 0 to 120. All groups show a rapid increase in concentration peaking around 12 hours, followed by a biphasic decline. The Severe group exhibits the highest peak concentration and the slowest clearance, reaching approximately 2800 ng/mL at 12 hours, while the Normal group peaks around 1000 ng/mL. By 48 hours, the Severe group maintains a concentration around 200 ng/mL, significantly higher than the Normal group (around 100 ng/mL).

The left chart shows AUC_{0-t} (ng\cdothr/mL). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 70000. AUC_{0-t} increases significantly with renal impairment severity. Normal group is approximately 12000 ng\cdothr/mL, Mild is approximately 18000 ng\cdothr/mL, Moderate is approximately 25000 ng\cdothr/mL, and Severe is approximately 55000 ng\cdothr/mL.
The right chart shows Oral Clearance (CL/F) (L/hr). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 20. CL/F decreases significantly with renal impairment severity. Normal group is approximately 13 L/hr, Mild is approximately 12 L/hr, Moderate is approximately 10 L/hr, and Severe is approximately 7 L/hr.

The data points show a strong negative correlation, indicating that as renal function (CLcr) decreases, the area under the curve (AUC0-t) increases significantly.

Urine (squares): Shows a rapid increase in cumulative excretion, reaching approximately 77% by 24 hours, and remaining relatively stable thereafter.

Total (triangles): Represents the sum of urine and feces excretion, showing a rapid increase, reaching approximately 97% by 24 hours, and remaining stable thereafter.

The 250 mg dose shows the highest activity change (around 200), followed by 50 mg (around 180), and 150 mg (around 40).
L3055: Bar chart showing the Mean (SE) Change in Urine GL-3 from baseline at week 12 and week 48 for three dose groups (50 mg, 150 mg, 250 mg). The Y-axis is 'Change in Urine GL-3 from baseline' (ranging from -400 to 400) and the X-axis is 'Dose (mg)'. Week 12 data (blue bars) shows a positive change for 50 mg and 150 mg doses, and a negative change for the 250 mg dose. Week 48 data (red bars) shows a positive change for the 150 mg dose, and a negative change for the 250 mg dose. The 50 mg dose shows minimal change at both time points.
L3056: Bar chart showing Mean (SE) Change in Kidney GL-3 from baseline at week 12 and week 48 for three doses (50 mg, 150 mg, 250 mg). The Y-axis is Change in Kidney GL-3 (ranging from -0.4 to 0.2). The X-axis is Dose (mg). Week 12 (dark bars) and Week 48 (red bars) are compared. At 50 mg, Week 48 change is approximately -0.25. At 150 mg, Week 48 change is approximately -0.20. At 250 mg, Week 48 change is approximately -0.05. Week 12 changes are generally near zero or slightly negative.
L3057: Bar chart showing Mean (SE) Change in Plasma GL-3 from baseline at week 12 and week 48 for three doses (50 mg, 150 mg, 250 mg). The Y-axis is Change in plasma GL-3 from baseline (ranging from 0.0 to 2.0). The X-axis is Dose (mg). Week 12 (dark bars) and Week 48 (red bars) are compared. At 50 mg, Week 48 change is approximately 1.6. At 150 mg, Week 48 change is approximately 1.0. At 250 mg, Week 48 change is approximately 1.0. Week 12 changes are generally near zero or slightly positive.
L3058: Figure 1 displays two representative chromatograms (b) (4) showing the analysis of AT1001 incubations with human hepatocytes at 1 \muM concentration. The top chromatogram is labeled "0 Hour Incubation" (Run 9), showing a single peak for AT1001 at Retention Time (RT) 16.125 and Area 10694.961. The bottom chromatogram is labeled "4 Hour Incubation" (Run 28), showing a single peak for AT1001 at RT 16.094 and Area 11394.954. The chromatograms indicate that AT1001 is stable over the 4-hour incubation period.

L3067: Figure 1A displays the Kaplan-Meier Survival Functions for Male Rats. The Y-axis represents Survival Probability (0.0 to 1.0) and the X-axis represents Survival time (week) (0 to 100). The legend indicates the Dose group:
0 mg/kg/day | Control 1
0 mg/kg/day | Control 2
50 mg/kg/day | Low
200 mg/kg/day | Mid
800/1200 mg/kg/day | High
The survival curves for all dose groups are very close together, showing a gradual decline in survival probability over time, with the High dose group exhibiting the lowest survival probability at later time points.
